           1.Introduction

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:   P | 6

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00     G21(SAZN XXX)

Azadegan South Oil Field is situated in West Karoun province on a large long-axis anticline with N-S trending with

approximate dimensions about 60 km width and 20 km length near to Iran-Iraq border, to the east of Yaran Oil Field

(70 km. west of Ahwaz city).

The trap area of the south Azadegan is limited to 43.2x17.2 km. The Azadegan Oil field was discovered in 1999,

when Well AZN-1 was drilled vertically down to the Cretaceous Fahliyan Formation with total depth of 4,643m. The

results of this well, revealed the reservoir characteristics of the field. Following to the result of the well no. AZN-1,

another 5 exploration wells was drilled in different parts of this huge field down to Fahliyan Formation. In the later

stage the well No.10 assumed as exploration well for evaluation of deeper formations. This well was drilled in 2008,

down to the depth of 5340m (Neyriz Fm.), mainly to evaluate the Jurassic formations.

Based on the exploration wells results, carbonate reservoir of Sarvak Formation is broadly developed in Azadegan

Field. This formation was penetrated in all of seven exploration wells (AZN-1, AZN-2, AZN-3, AZN-4, AZN-5, AZN-

6 and AZN-10). Clastic oil-bearing zones of Kazhdumi Formation developed in main anticline of Azadegan Field,

extending from north to center of South Azadegan. The Clastic oil-bearing zones of Gadvan Formation developed

only in crest area and south parts of South Azadegan Filed, which was discovered by AZN-1 in crest and AZN-4 in

south parts. The carbonates of Fahliyan Formation also developed in Azadegan field, but the oil reservoir of this

formation developed in South part of South Azadegan.

The Azadegan Field is an elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belonging to "Arabian Trend".

The stratigraphy sequence of the field includes AghaJari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak,

Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan Formations, from top to bottom. This sequence in well no AZN-10, the

deepest up to now drilled well of the field, continues with Garau, Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and Neyriz

formations.

The Dominant lithofacies of Sarvak Formation are Rudist-bearing packstone and partially grainstone, which contains

abundant bioclasts. Some other facies like matrix-dominated wackestone and mudstone are also found.

South Azadegan oil field in Area-III.
The location of this well is situated amongst the AZNS-006, AZNS-113 ST1, AZNS-23, AZNS60, AZN-12 and
AZNS-085 and it is planned to be drilled as a Vertical well.

2.4.Off-set Information

2.4.1 Summary of Drilling History SAZN-085

 26” Hole Section
Spud rig @ 1393.07.16, drilling to 152m, run 20” casing to 150m and cement job as program with 60bbl
cement return

 17-1/2” Hole Section
Drilling to 1277m, while increase mud weigh from 67 to 85pcf, run 13-3/8” CSG and cement job as
program with 180bbl cement return.

 12-1/4” Section Summary

Drilling to 3237m with 6-150bph loss while pump lcm pill and by pass system, take full set log, continue
drilling to 3367m with 20-150bph loss while pump lcm pill and by pass system, complete loss pump lcm pill
several time, decrease mud weigh from 80 to 77pcf, hole condition and while condition stuck string and
complete loss, work on stuck and free pipe, pump lcm pill and decrease mud weigh to 70pcf, drilling to
3661m flow the well, increase mud weigh to 78pcf, drilling to 3950m, take log as program, run 9-5/8” casing
and cement job as program, no cement return.

 8-1/2” Section Summary
Drilling to 3957m, take CBL/VDL/CCL/GR log from 9-5/8” casing, drilling to
4034m, increase mud weigh to 101pcf due to flow well, drilling to 4090m (TD)
while increase mud weigh to 103pcf , run 7” liner to 4090m, pressure test and
inject 150bbl cement in 9-5/8”casing at 3103m, dry test 7” liner lap ok, take
CBL/VDL/CCL/GR log from 7” liner, set cement plug f/ 3882 to 3650m and test
9-5/8”casing not ok, take cast log from 3648-1200m in 9-5/8” casing, observe tear
9-5/8”casing from 3157 to 3140m, tie back 7” liner to 3829m, dry test 7” liner lap
not ok, tie back 7” liner lap to 2903m, set cement plug f/2903 to 2763m,
suspension well without completion string and release rig at 1395/05/27.

2.4.2 Lesson Learned
Never run in hole un-inspection string to avoid wash out and fishing problems.
All main dimension of each tools and BHA (such as OD, ID, and Fish Neck …) shall be measured before RIH.
Use high quality of equipment to save time and cost such as cement plugs and casing accessories.
All tools will be used in operation shall be have suitable back such as casing running and dry test tools.
In 17 ½” hole section specially after 800m, to reduce pipe sticking and massive hole sloughing, keep mud water
loss as low. In this section usually the well is gauge and 40% excess for lead cement slurry is enough.
Due to short 12 ¼” interval it’s highly recommended prepare all equipment and mud and cement and additives
before start this section.
Due to high wash out in Ilam & Laffan formation it is need to consider 200% excess for 7” liner open hole cement
and also observance centralizer placement to make good cement quality

3.2.Stratigraphy

The area is completely covered by the alluvium of Holocene age; the drillable layers will be Aghajari, Gachsaran,

Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan formations. A brief stratigraphic

sequence has been predicted as follows (Table 2):

       -   Aghajari Formation (surface to 974.1mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 964.1 m)
The Aghajari Formation consists of a repetition of brown to grey calcareous sandstones, chert debris and gypsum-

veined red marl. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.. Aghajari

overlays Gachsaran Formation with a conformable boundary.

       -  Gachsaran Formation (974.1-1280.9 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 306.8 m)
The Gachsaran Formation thickness ranges from 350 to 500m. Deposition of the Gachsaran Formation is cyclic.

The cycle began with a deposition of shallow-water marine carbonates, and then it was overlain by a thick evaporitic

phase. The evaporates were capped by red to variegated marl, which represented the end of cycle. Gachsaran is

laid on Asmari Formation with a comformable boundary.

       -   Asmari Formation (1280.9-1474.1mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 193.2 m)
The Asmari Formation in Azadegan Field developed a massive sandstone interval, with a few thin argillaceous

limestones in the upper sections. Sandstone are clear, fine to medium-sorted, and poorly consolidated. On the

other hand, the lower sections consist of intercalation of sandy, chalky, slightly dolomitic high-energy limestone with

sandstone layers. Asmari is parallel to Pabdeh Formation at the bottom.

       -  Pabdeh/Jahrum Formation (1474.1-1624.6 mMD)(Drilled Thickness: 150.5m)
Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown

argillaceous limestones and marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower

part is composed of dark-brown, slightly argillaceous limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia,

indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene

Formation exists in the Azadegan area. Chert and cherty limestone layers at the bottom of Pabdeh Formation are

very important for drilling parameters on the PDC bit (after drilling 140 m inside Pabdeh Formation the cherty layers

maybe appeared). Jahrum Formation will be distinguished here in this well at the depth interval between 1624.6 –

1865.9 mMD with a thickness about 241.3 m.

       -  Pabdeh Formation (1865.9-2123.9 mMD)(Drilled Thickness: 258.0m)

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:   P | 28

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00     G21(SAZN XXX)

Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown

argillaceous limestones and marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower

part is composed of dark-brown, slightly argillaceous limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia,

indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in age.

       -   Gurpi Formation (2123.9-2538.9mMD)(Drilled Thickness: 415.0 m)
Gurpi Formation is consisted of argillaceous limestone with higher clay content rather than Pabdeh Formation.

About 85 meters of pure limestone will be observed in the middle part of the formation in this area which divides it

into 3 different parts as below:

       -  Upper Gurpi (2123.9 -2314.3mMD)(Drilled Thickness 190.4m)
The Gurpi Formation in the Azadegan Field consists of grey to dark-brownish, slightly argillaceous micritic limestone

with Maestrichtian to Campanian aged fauna. At Well AZN-01, about 50 meters of chalky limestones were

encountered in the middle of the Gurpi formation, which contains abundant fragments of rudists, bryozoa, corals,

and Orbitoides.

       -   Tarbur Member (2314.3-2398.5mMD)(Drilled Thickness 84.2 m)
In the western Abadan Plain about 50 meters thick chalky limestone was encountered in the middle part of the

Gurpi Formation, which  contains abundant fragments  of  bioclasts, suggesting very shallow  depositional

environment. This interval is equivalnt to the Tarbur Formation with reservoir quality in other regions.

       -  Lower Gurpi (2398.5-2538.9mMD)(Drilled Thickness 140.4 m)
This member consists of gray, light gray, medium hard argillaceous Limestone. The Gurpi formation is overlain

directly on Ilam formation by conformity contact.

       -   Ilam Formation (2538.9-2613.2mMD)(Drilled Thickness:74.3 m)
In the Azadegan Field, the Ilam Formation consists of grey micritic limestone, often argillaceous, rarely pyritic,

limestones. Microfauna, mostly pelagic, including rotalids and oligostegina assemblage, a few algae, bryozoa, and

rudist fragments exist.

       -   Laffan Member (2613.2-2620.3mMD)(Drilled Thickness: 7.1m)
The Laffan Formation developed a thin greenish-grey pyritic shale layer with ostracods fauna that capping the

Sarvak Formation.

       -   Sarvak Formation (2620.3-3260.9mMD)(Drilled Thickness: 640.6m)

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:   P | 29

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00     G21(SAZN XXX)

The Sarvak Formation developed a carbonate unit, which was gradually overlying the Kazhdumi Formation, and it

is abruptly terminated by an erosion phase, covered by a various late Cretaceous sediment. The lithology of Sarvak

consists of the dark grey fine grained nodular argillaceous limestone interbedded with thin marls in the bottom. The

Limestone graded upward into a white-to-buff massive chalky limestone with siliceous nodules; developed a very

massive tan-to-brown limestone containing abundant rudist debris in the middle part. In the upper part is made up

of rubbly limestone with red staining and leached zones of breccia and the top of Sarvak Formation is very

ferruginous.

       -  Kazhdumi Formation (3260.9-3464.6mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 203.7m)
Kazhdumi Formation begins with dark brown, soft to medium hard, some fissile and calcareous Shale. This

formation is start with dark brown to dark green gray, medium hard to hard, soft to medium, high bituminous,

calcareous Shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone, sandy claystone, claystone, marl, sandy limestone,

limestone beds. The Argillaceous Limestone is dark brown to brown gray, medium hard to hard, micro bituminous.

The Sandy claystone is light green gray, soft, high calcareous. The Claystone is gray to light gray, soft. The Marl is

green gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Limestone is dark green gray, hard, faint sandy. The Limestone is light gray to

green gray, medium hard to soft, partly argillaceous. The Kazhdumi Formation at base is consisting of Azadegan

Member.

       -   Dariyan Formation (3464.6-3627.1mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 162.5m)
In Dariyan Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones

were deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but

not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were

defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are

consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan formation divided to three part:Dariyan formation starts with white to white cream grey clean limestone

type I/II, I. Base of Dariyan formation, appears grey, green Pyritic shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone.
This zone called transition zone and thickness is 16mt. after last grey limestone indicates of Gadvan formation.
This carbonate formation contains three zones. D1 and D3 are non-reservoirs and D2 is a moderately poor reservoir.

       -  Gadvan Formation (3627.1– 3809.5mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 182.5 +m)
In This Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones were

deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but not

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:   P | 30

                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00     G21(SAZN XXX)

very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were

defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are

consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected.

In the Azadegan Field the Gadvan Formation is divided into three part;

Upper Gadvan; the upper part starts with occurrence of a terrigenous rock. The lithology is composed of grey

claystone and some shale.

Khalij Mbr.; appears with pure limestone, cream, light brown and in some intervals oil stain.

Lower Gadvan; consisting of alternating of marl, argillaceous limestone, limestone, claystone, sandy claystone

and sandstone beds. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky, slightly calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is gray

white gray medium hard to soft, partly pyritic. The Limestone is white, white to light gray, hard. The Claystone is

light gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky, fine sand grained. The Sandstone is brown to

gray, partly white buff, gray,  friable, sorted, sub rounded, medium quartz grained, partly calcareous. This

sandstones which occur in Zone-3 and Zone-5 are the reservoir of this formation and in this part is equivalent with

Kushk member.

3.3.Requierment for Data Acquisition by Mud Logging

3.3.1 Mud Logging Activities in SAZN-xxx (G-21)

Mud logging unit will be spudded at the start of the drilling operation containing all standard sensors for controlling

the activities. In geological operation, the required mud logging activities are as below:

-      Depth controlling

-      Lag depth calculation through mud Pump GPM and drilling depth

-     Gas samples gathering, analysis and giving the related reports

-        Oil show samples gathering and including the results in daily reports.

-       Collaboration with directional drilling section in order for performing related calculations

-       Catching the cutting samples, describe the samples and providing graphic logs

-       Providing daily, weekly and monthly reports according to the company formats

-       Preparing the Well Geological Final Report according to the standard formats.

3.3.3 Gas Logging
        The Gas Logging shall be in action continuously since the second spud-in, recording the followings with
        10 ppm accuracy
      ➢  Total Gas
      ➢ Gas Chromatograph (C1; C2; C3: iC4/nC4; iC5/nC5)
      ➢  Non-Hydrocarbon Gas (H2S and CO2)
      ➢  Accuracy: within 10 ppm.

3.3.4 Drillling Parameters Logging
        The Drilling Parameters logging shall be in action continuously since the spud in. Contractor`s
         Personnel shall continuously measure, record, and display the following drilling parameters (But Not
         Limited to)
      ➢  Weight on Bit (Ton/Klb)
      ➢ Hook Load (Ton/Klb)
      ➢  Rotary Torque (ft-lbs)
      ➢  Rotary Speed (rpm)
      ➢  Standpipe Pressure (psi)
      ➢  Casing Pressure (psi)
      ➢  Block Height (m)

        The Following drilling fluid parameters shall be continuously measured and record and displayed:
      ➢ Mud weight in and out (PCF/sg)
      ➢ Mud Temperature in and out (deg C/F)
      ➢ Mud flow rate in and out (gpm)
      ➢  Pit VVolume Monitoring
      ➢  Trip Tank Volume

4.2.Well Construction

4.2.1 Proposed Casing Program in SAZN-G-21

Following casing program is proposed for the G-21 well location:

    •   20” Surface Casing: Surface casing shoe will be set at 150m to seal off the shallow stratum.
    •   13 3/8” Intermediate Casing: Aghajari to Gachsaran formations will be drilled and 13 3/8" casing shoe will be set
      5m above top of Amari formation which will be specified by well-site geologist.
    •   9 5/8” Intermediate Casing: Asmari to Ilam formations will be drilled and 9 5/8" casing shoe will be set at top of
        Ilam formation which will be specified by well-site geologist.
    •   7” Production Liner: Formations of Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi and Gadvan will be drilled and 7” liner shoe will
       be set at TD which will be specified by well-site geologist after acquisition and evaluation of Petrophysical logs. 60m
        overlap 7” liner with 9 5/8” casing must be considered.

       Proposed Casing / Liners for G-21 (PAD-79)

4.4.Drilling Sequence by Hole section

4.4.1 Rig Move
      ➢  Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:
                       •  Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.
                       •  Safe standby areas for rig
                       •   In case move have to be suspended.
                       •  Loss of trucks in case of repair.
                       •  Moving at night.
      ➢  Hold pre-move safety meeting. Ensure everyone understands individual
                    responsibilities:
      ➢ Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.
      ➢ Commence rig move to SAZN# (G-21), PAD NO. 79

                       •   Position rig for optimal operations
      ➢  Rig up on cellar
                       •   Finish rig up and commence operational procedure for SAZN-XXX (G-
                              21)
                       •   Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.
      ➢  Test all manifold and surface safety valves
                       •  RKB – GLE and Elevation
                       •  Spud date and time.
      ➢ Mud tanks should be empty during move. Fill water tanks and keep as full as possible.

                                TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:      P | 56

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00      G21(SAZNXXX)

4.4.2 Pre spud

Certificate 
    All rig equipment is on location, properly rigged up and function-tested. Check availability and content
    of hoisting equipment inspection & maintenance records according to API.

Safety Device 
   Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report; test derrick man
   escape device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on daily drilling report; test
     rig intercom system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-Mud Logger communication link. 
   Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g. fire extinguishers, eye-wash stations etc., is installed at the
    proper locations as per rig layout diagrams. 
   Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support drilling unit during whole well
    period and that mast is centred above rotary table. 
   Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig floor hoisting equipment
    are adequate for handling 8″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure that mechanical tong torque gauges
     for drill pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.

Rig Equipment 
   Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed, and 26″ bit breakers are
   on the rig floor. Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools available for vertical section survey. 
  Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been function-tested with
    charging pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate screen inventory for the
    shale shakers is available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure water tanks are completely
     filled and transfer lines from water tanks to mud system are hooked up. Make sure all mud materials
   and chemicals required to drill 26″ section are on location, in sound condition and suitably stored.
   Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and sufficient volume has been built for top section and for
     well-kill. 
  Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts: liners, pistons, valves,
    seats etc. Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with Nitrogen to the proper pre-charge
    pressure, 30% of expected standpipe pressure during drilling of the 26″ hole section; check if sufficient
    Nitrogen bottles are available on location to adjust pulsation dampener pre-charge pressure as
    required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off valves are set at the proper pressure for the liner sizes
    installed and that the mud discharge lines from the pop-off valves to the mud tanks are properly
    secured. Check that all high-pressure hoses are properly secured with safety chains. P/T should be
   done to high-pressure surface line of circulating system
   .
Wellhead & BOP 
   Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m above cellar bottom to
    drain cement from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface casing. Verify that pre-installed
    30″ conductor pipe is vertical and that no angle/bend exists between conductor and 30″ riser pipe. 
  Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and ready to be run, float shoe
    should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on the pipe-rack prior to spud in the
     well. 
   Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested properly.

Others 
   Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig consumption available on site. 
  Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is ok. 
   Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location. Discuss procedures
    involved throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and critical safety operation measures. 
  NIDC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed it in rigs. The checklist will be performed based on
    the aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following matters should be checked:
        
      Check & observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in four stages; rig levelling,
         derrick verticality, derrick alignment with rotary table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe.
      To do this pick up 3 joints of 9 ½" DC and check the verticality of the derrick. 
       Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as :
               1. Desander, Desilter, mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers, mud guns &
                mixers, should be all available on rig and functioning properly.
               2. All mud tanks, mud transfer lines & connection should be checked by filling them with
               water & cross wellhead circulation.
               3. Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing equipment.
             The calibration should be done under supervision of well site drilling supervisor before
                  starting the operation.
               4. Weight indicator, rotary speed gauge, any smart drilling instrumentation, Top Drive,
               Stroke meter, pressure gauges on manifold and in the drilling console, loss / gain gauge
             and any other gauges should be checked and calibrated.
               5. Stand pipe manifold valves & connection must be checked & pressure tested.
               6. All motors should be checked under load prior to start drilling
               7. Check & control mud stock & cement stock every day &  if there is any necessity to
               request mud material, cement or any drilling tool, it should be prepared & requested at
               proper time.
Note: Fill up following forms and send to drilling office:
  ➢  Pre-spud check list
  ➢  Location Handed Over

4.4.3 26” Section
          After commencing spud-in, 26” hole drilling will be started. The drilling would be anticipated to
        go on fast.

            Casing Point                20” Casing @ 150m
            Formation                   Aghajari
            Risks & Hole
                                                     Bit balled up            problems
             Deviation                                 INCLINOMETER (TOTCO)
              control
            Logging                              ---
               Bit Type                     1-1-1 (1-1-5)
                                                      Drilling Water/Bentonite mud

                  MW      65-68               Drilling Fluid

                                              Active Mud >70                                  Vis
                                               Hi-Vis Pill >90
                    WOB     10-30
               Drilling
                     GPM     300-1000            Parameters
                     RPM    Max available

    Sequence of Operation

   •  Make up 26” BHA as per BHA design. Tag formation at ±14m.
   •    Drill ahead formation down to 151m (20” casing point in Aghajari formation) using Hi-vis bentonite
            pill. Pay enough attention to drill straight hole and use low GPM & high gel mud around conductor
        pipe shoe to avoid loss and any washout below constraction.

        Note:
        o  In Case of complete loss while drilling 26” hole, cure loss by pump LCM pill or
             cement as cement plug.
        o  TD should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole below casing shoe.
        o  The exact TD will be determined based on casing tally.
        o  Run Inclinometer (Totco) each 50m to evaluate the verticality of the hole.
        o  Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site and are in service able
                  condition.
        o  Ensure that adequate fishing tools for this section of hole are available.
        o  Enough Mud Material, LCM and cement should be available on rig site to use in case of
                needing.
        o  At TD, Circulate and clean the hole. Spot high viscosity pill at bottom, drop Inclinometer
                 (Totco) POOH to surface. Check Bit.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                   South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                        Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

    Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
   CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00      G21(SAZNXXX)

•  Perform hole condition (using reamer 26” if needed) prior to run casing. If observe any indication
    of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and ream to make sure that the hole condition is good.
•  Perform safety meeting.
•  R/U casing running equipment.
•  Run ±150m (Approx.± 15 Jts) 20", 94 ppf, K-55, BTC casing with stab-in duplex shoe to bottom
   as per casing program. Casing shoe should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole and enough stick
   up (1 m on rig floor). Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud.
     Note:
      o  Take care when running casing not to set down excessive weight if tight hole or ledges
            are encountered.
•  Run stinger on DP’s using the “C” Plate & 5” x 20” centralizer. Sting in. M/U & test cementing
     lines. Circulate for one casing annulus volume. While circulating, check the DPs annulus for any
    return. Use all DPs weight on the string if observing any minor leakage through DPs annulus.
•  Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done
     in due time. Displace with mud (1-2 bbls Short Displace). Monitor for pressures. Record and report
    the final pressure, Sting out. Check the float. POOH one stand, Circulate to flush/clean drill pipe.
•   After cement returns to cellar floor, open the 2" nipple on conductor & flush it.
•  POOH to surface. L/D stinger.
•  Keep the casing on the elevator until cement surface samples get hard. Check for casing to be
    center inside conductor pipe.

     Note:
      o   After cement job, if float of shoe is not ok and cement return inside casing, pull out the
            hole drill pipes very fast and don’t fill anullus to let fill it with cement top job.
      o  Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top
              jobs.
      o    If there is no cement return to surface, a top job should be performed to fill the annulus.
          Use same slurry weight as the primary cement slurry. Ensure the availability of adequate
            X-over for connecting line pipe to cement line. The cement should be accelerated with
           max. (2%) Calcium Chloride BWOC and no other additives. Wait for the top job cement
              to get hard.
•  Check casing by slack off weight.
•  Cut conductor pipe. Rough cut landing joint and L/D C.P. and L.J. Dress and bevel 20” suitable
    to install and to weld casing head housing.
•   Install and weld casing head housing 20 ¾” – 3000 psi on casing and pressure test the sealing of
   welded casing. (Pressure test CHH port seal to a maximum of 80% of the collapse strength
    of the casing or maximum 400 psi for 15 min). Outlets must be aligning with cellar stairs.
     Note:
      o  Before installing the casing head housing, open all the test ports and clean inside it with
            water and air.
      o  Check the 20 ¾” wear bushing, 20 ¾” Plug Tester and 13-3/8” Casing Slip and seal
           assembly in CHH before installing CHH (on Surface).
      o  The distance between its top flange and ground level must be 2.50m.
•  N/U 21 ¼”-2000 BOP stack and related lines and valves and test all to 1500psi.
•  N/U Bell Nipple & flow line
•  P/U & M/U Next BHA and stand drill pipes to 1358m.
•    Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:
     Casing Report

4.4.4 17-1/2” Section

        Casing Point                    13-3/8” Casing @ ± 1276 m

        Formation                        Aghajari, Gachsaran
        Risks & Hole                     Tight holes, wash & ream (especially in pipe connections),
        problems                      minor stucks and bit balling, Hole Deviation

         Deviation control               Single Shot every 100m & Multi Shot every 300m

        Logging                  Open hole; CDR/GR/Caliper

          Bit Type                          1-1-5, 1-3-5, PDC (S223)

                                      Gel Mud & KCl/Pol Mud
          Drilling Fluid     MW      67-88 pcf
                                    Vis.     40-55
                   WOB   Maximum Available
          Drilling Parameters  RPM    120-180
                    GPM    800-1000
        Formation Sampling
        sample would be taken Every 5 meters.
       NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.

       Sequence of Operation
   •  Make up 17-1/2” BHA as per BHA Design. RIH, tag top of cement, Drill out cement & shoe and
         rat hole cement. Work on junk sub.
   •  Watch carefully for any indication of abnormal torque, since there might be a possibility of shoe
       being backed off. Report problem immediately.
   •   Continue drilling 3m below shoe. Clean out the rat hole, circulate clean.
   •    Drill ahead formation down to 25m below 20” casing shoe with hi-vis pill.
   •   Circulate and clean hole, POOH to surface and P/U the same bit & S. Stab @ 20m according to
     BHA program, RIH to bottom.
   •    Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to 550 m. CBU.
   •   Condition trip to 150 m, Run back to btm
   •   Continue drilling to 750 m, displace mud in hole by 74 pcf KCL/Polymer.
   •   Continue drilling to 950 m,
   •    circ to clean hole, POOH to surface.
   •  P/U PDC bit and Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to casing point @ ±1276.5 m, 5m TVD
      Above Top of ASMARI formation meanwhile perform condition trip each 300 m.

     Note:
      o    If use stabilizer to drill out shoe track make sure the RPM & WOB are in low as possible
                until the stabilizer is out from shoe.

      o  Choke manifold and choke line must be lined up for SOFT SHUT-IN through pit or
            burning line.

         pump LCM Pills to cure losses.
•   At section TD, circulate, clean hole. Perform wiper trip to shoe and observe well @ shoe. RIH,

   GPM.

•  Casing shoe depth should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough
     stick up (1.5 m) on rig floor.

    cementing lines. Circulate for 120% of casing annulus volume.
•  Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done
     in due time. Displace with mud. Monitor for pressures.
•  Bump plug with 1500 psi (Max 80% Casing Burst) / 15 min as CIT (In case of not observing
   bumping during CMT job, perform CITW/1500psi (Max 80% Casing Burst)/15min with consider
   mud weight after D/O CMT 7m above F. Shoe also consider MW while perform CIT), Record and
    report the final pressure, Bleed off pressure, Check for back flow, if any back flow observed, hold
    the pressure inside casing until the cement get hard.
•  Hold on casing string weight. Drop casing hanger. Slack off 80% of casing string weight. Test
    hanger to 800-1000 psi pressure. (Open base flange side outlet valves while testing).
•  Remove the flow line and diverter. Rough cut L.J. Dress and bevel casing neck.
•   Install CSG head spool 20 ¾”-3000*13 ⅝”-5000, Pack p-seals and pressure test p-seals and
    cavity with maximum 80% of rated collapse pressure
     Note:
      o  Ensure that the casing is drifted; cleaned and tallied priors to RIH.
      o   Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud.
      o  Use Bow Type centralizer each 5 jts in open-hole and at least 2 no. Rigid type centralizer
               in cased-hole. (In shale/sticky Marl layers use bow types each 10 jts)
      o  Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site and are in serviceable
              condition.
      o  Ensure that enough amount and type of LCM & CMT are available

      sub and take care while drill shoe track to avoid bit damage.

      planned mud weight for 12-1/4” hole section plus 2 PCF)

     influx), Spot 70 bbl hi-vis Pill prior to POOH.

    excessive solid percent can reduce ROP significantly and cause major drilling difficulties. So, it’s

 Drilling Parameters          RPM     100-180
                      GPM     400-550

   o  NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.

Well site geologist is fully responsible to determine the frequency of cutting sampling, and mud logging

crew shall take sample based on well site geologist’s advice. In deviated/Highly Deviated interval the

sampling shouldn’t be limited to 20 m, and the well site geologist should take care of sampling and

collecting sample of any cuttings with different characteristics

       Sequence of Operations
   •  Pick up 8 ½” bit and bit sub, RIH and drill out cement and shoe track, and shoe.
   •   Drilling 8 1/2" hole (3-5m) to 2542 m, ROP: 1-2 m/hr.
   •  Work on junk sub, Circulate and clean hole, POOH to Surf.
   •  R/U logging equipment, Run SBT (RBT) /CCL/GR/CBL/VDL or equivalent F/9 5/8" CSG SHOE
         to 13 3/8" CSG shoe, POOH & R/D logging Equip.
   •  P/U & M/U New 8 1/2" PDC bit (Optional performance) and S. Stab @ 20m, RIH to bottom.
        Displace hole with new mud, perform FIT. (EMW should be equal to max. planned mud weight

         for 8-1/2” hole section plus 2 PCF
   •  Perform break circulation
   •   Drilling 8-1/2" hole down to 3809.5 mMD,5 m TVD inside Gadvan-8 (7" liner point),
   •  Run SBT (RBT) /CCL/GR/CBL/VDL or CAST/ USIT as per program, POOH & R/D logging
        Equip.
   •  R/U casing running equipment. Run and hang 7" liner from ±2479 mMD to ±3809.5 mMD, (Hung
      ±60-80m above 9 5/8" CSG shoe).

•     It is recomendeded ues SPIR-O-LIZER & SPIR-O-LOCK for liners instead of bow centralizers to
    increase stand off and also reduce risk of stuck
•  M/U and torque up the shoe-to-shoe joint (29ppf, L-80 (1), gas tight premium connection). F/U
      first joint then P/U & M/U second joint of 7” liner, run & F/U same. Break circulation and function

     test shoe.
•  P/U float Collar, clean threads, M/U and use baker lock for its box. M/U 1 joint 7” liner (29ppf, L-
   80 (1), gas tight premium connection, special drift) above float Collar & run & F/U same, break

    circulation and check float valve. P/U LDC, clean threads, M/U and use baker lock for its box, add

   one more 7” liner (29ppf, L-80 (1), gas tight premium connection), Break circulation and check

     float valve.
•  Run 7” liner as per Tally, F/U all 5 first joints, and then F/U every 5 joint. M/U liner hanger assembly
   as per job specialist instruction.
•  Record pick-up and slack-off weight of liner.
•  RIH 1 stand DP. Break & continue circulation as 1.2 cap of liner string. Check liner hanger for
    leaks.
•  R/D liner running equipment
•   Continue RIH on DP’s while drifting DP’s. F/U each 10 stands. With the liner assembly shoe at 9
    5/8” casing shoe, break circulation and check free circulation through the liner assembly. Circulate

    at least 1.2 volume of Liner + Drill Pipe capacity.
•   Continue RIH to liner setting depth, wash down last stand to bottom. Tag bottom. Continue
    circulation for at least 120% of the liner and DP’s volume. Space out the string to have cement

    manifold at proper position.
•   Circulation test at following flow rates: 20, 30, 40, 50 SPM, checking for losses, record results in
   IADC and advice DSV.
•  Record pick-up and slack-off weight of liner at bottom.
•  Drop ball and set liner hanger as per job specialist instruction. Test hanger setting in place. Shear
     ball catcher and establish circulation. Release setting tool from liner and make sure that it’s free,

   as per job specialist procedure.

    Note: in case if it is not possible to set liner hanger, set liner shoe in TD then release liner hanger
    setting tools
•  Cement liner as per program. Perform liner integrity test w/ 3000 psi /15 min while cementing
   (max 80% of liner burst with consider mud weight)
•  POOH and L/D setting tools, RIH w/ 8-1/2" Bit (8RR1), confirm & clean Liner Lap. POOH to Surf.

•  Check the rig pump efficiency in order to prevent any kind of failure in the cementing job.
•   Surge tank, cement unit, batch mixer and dry cement lines should be checked before the
    cementing job commencement in presence of well site supervisor, OIM and cement supervisor to

    prevent any blockage during the cement job. (Get a confirmation from well site supervisor)
•     Drill water, spacer, slurry solution and dry cement samples from each silo should be taken during
    the job and sent to lab for analyzing.
•   Check the pit for spacer mixing to be clean, to prevent any contamination.
•   Amount of cement and additives to be calculated based on the final cementing program.
•   Double check the calculation with the well site supervisor (or well site engineer) before the job.
•   Perform risk assessment and discuss contingency plan with the well site supervisor.
•   Any deviation to the design (volume, rate, fluid sequence) MUST be notified to the cementing
    engineer for risk assessment.

     Operation Sequence

•   Set liner hanger
•    Circulate one full hole volume mud for conditioning the hole
•   Perform a Pre-Job safety meeting with all personnel involved on the operation as well as the
     client representative. Read and follow the risk assessment for this job, Ensure communication

   between cement compant and rig floor
•   Pressure test cement line to 500 psi for 5 minutes then slowly increase the pressure to 4500 psi
     for 10 min and release the pressure to the cementing unit
•  Pump Chemical wash
•  Pump spacer
•  Pump cement slurry
•   Release pump down plug
•  Pump 3 bbl cement slurry (as a part of displacement)
•   Displace cement slurry with mud (decrease flow rate to 4 BPM for last 10 bbl displacement
    volume).
•  P/U & M/U 6-1/8" Bit & new BHA (4-3/4" DC & 3-1/2" DP) & RIH, drill POB and clean out inside
    7" Liner to LDC, Circ. Clean the hole.
•  POOH to 10m Above L. Lap, perform 7" L. Lap press test (w/ 2000psi), POOH to surface. Perform
    scraper trip for dry test.

4.4.7 Well Flowing

   o The main objectives of the 96hrs cleanup and well flowing is to displace completion fluid, possible
     mud losses, and flow back the reservoir fluid until achieving the cleanup operation criteria. The
      cleanup operation procedure by rig is categorized as follows:

   o Rigging up all related-equipment and perform hydro-test
   o Retrieving barriers (plug, TWV) and landing protection sleeve in TRSSSV nipple
   o Displacing the wellbore via CT with suitable method and compatible fluid
   o Flowing the well over 96hrs thru different choke sizes while testing integrity and surface sampling
   o Retrieving protection sleeve, inject diesel to top of 3SV, Close TRSSSV, Set BPV in tubing hanger,
      secure well & release rig.

                                TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:      P | 74

4.5.Well Design

4.5.1 Material Selection

Casing
Corrosive Environment Analysis and Strength Check
Based on the data of offset wells in this oilfield, there is a certain degree of corrosive problems in the 4
layers to be produced. Corrosive medium are principally composed of 2 parts: one is the corrosive gases
like H2S and CO2 existing in the produced fluid; and the other is the high salinity formation water. The
contents of main corrosive fluids in offset wells are shown in Table

                               H2S and CO2 Content

                                       CO2 from      H2S from     CO2 from Separator
               Reservoir      H2S from PVT
    Well No                            PVT (mol%)    Seperator    (mol%)               formation      (mol%)
                                                        (mol%)
   AZN2      Sarvak          0.13            6.71           7.93/150psi    0.06/150psi
              Sarvak          0.06            5.44           0.02/150      6.32
   AZN4     Kazhdumi      0               2.62          0             2.99/150psi
             Gadvan                         2.64
   AZN6     Gadvan                         2.47                          2.566/400psi

    1)  Corrosion in this oilfield is a result of combined action of H2S, CO2 and high salinity formation
        water. The corrosive mechanism is complicated, and the corrosive rate on the casing is difficult
         to determine. Based on the NACE standard and the data from the Table, the producers result in
       severe corrosive environment. However, by the research on development conditions and
        technical indices, it can be found that although the corrosive fluid contents are high in the oilfield,
        the corrosive environment is not extremely serious as the following reasons:

    2)  In general, the temperature that cause the SSC/SCC is concentrated in the range from 20℃ to
      30℃. The average depth of the horizontal section is 2,730m with the reservoir temperature of
      96℃, and tensional stress on the casing does not exist in the horizontal section. Therefore, the
        influence of SSC/SCC may not be considered for the casing/liner.

    3)  Based on the RMDP, the production GOR in the first development phase should not be higher
       than the initial GOR, therefore, the flowing pressure is higher than the bubble point pressure all
        the time, that is, degassing problems do not exist in the horizontal section and some vertical
        section, the H2S and CO2 have no chance of separation out of oil phase, and  it is lack of
        prerequisite for casing corrosion.

    4)  4Based on above all, the corrosive environment of the casing string is not severe. The optimal
        selection for the casing string can be stated briefly as follows.

                                TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:      P | 75

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       00      G21(SAZNXXX)

Material Selection of Casing String

1) In this oilfield, the corrosion of production string obviously occurs in 2 regions. One is the region where
the pressure is above the bubble point; in which degassing problems do not exist, and the corrosion
environment is relatively weak. Another one is the region where the pressure is below the bubble point;
because of the decline of pressure, the concentration of corrosive gas will increase and the corrosion is
relatively serious. Considering the corrosive environment is not serious in the low water cut production
stage and the production practice in this oilfield has proved the L80 is fit for it, So, the L80 should be taken
as the material of 9-5/8″ casing, 7″, 5 1/2″ and 4-1/2″ liner. In order to prevent the hazard of gas leakage
after production degassing occurs, 9-5/8″ casing, 7″ and 4-1/2″ liner should be Gas tight thread. 2) The
110S is selected as the material of 9-5/8″/7″/4-1/2″ casing rather than L-80 due to the high strength
requirement in Fahliyan wells, what’s more the 110S is fit for sour gas environment.

Wellhead
          1)  Selection of material class of Wellhead & X-mas Tree:

                                 H2S & CO2 in Offset Wells

                                Reservoir   H2S from PVT   CO2 from                    Well No                                formation      (mol%)     PVT (mol%)

                 AZN2       Sarvak          0.13           6.71
                               Sarvak          0.06           5.44
                 AZN4     Kuzhdumi        0             2.62
                            Gadvan                         2.64
                 AZN6      Gadvan                         2.47

Considering the information above, API 6A 19th & NACE MR-0175 (2003) /ISO 1516, and Cl- content
which is being most concerned, the South AZADEGA X-mas tree material and the tubing hanger should
be FF0.5 class.As to the casing head, it’s barely exposed to resident fluid, or only for quite a short time,
when it comes to desert working condition, according to API 6A19th & NACE-MR-0175 (2003) /ISO 1516,
the tubing spool should be EE0.5, the casing head material class should be DD0.5 ( ANSI 4130) , stainless
steel cladding in ring groove

          2)  Selection of PSL (product specification level)

According to API 6A 19th, The final PSL for wellhead and X-mas tree are as below:

                           PSL for wellhead and X-mas tree

                                    Item               Specification
                      Casing Head Housing
                                                    PSL-2                     assembly
                      Casing spool assembly           PSL-2
                      Tubing spool assembly           PSL-3
                      Tubing hanger                PSL-3G
                        X-Tree assembly               PSL-3G

          3)  Selection of PR (Performance Requirement)

According to API Specification 6A 19th edition, performance requirements are specific nd unique to the
product  in the as-shipped condition.  All products shall be designed to perform according to the
requirements of API 6A clauses 4 and clauses 10 while in the pressure and temperature ranges and
corrosion envirement. Other requirements include load capability, cycles, and operating force or torque.
There are two performance requirement levels: PR1 and PR2. Valves operating as safety valves shall be
of the PR2 performance level and meet the requirements of Annex I in API 6A For SA wells, the guideline
for PR selection is that all parts not contacting well fluids in nomal production period are PR1, all parts
contacting well fluids are PR2 as follow, Casing head housing and casing head spool assembly - PR1.
Tubing head spool, tubing hanger and X-mas tree - PR2.

4.5.5 Mud Program

26” Section from Surface to 150 m

This section will be drilled as Gel mud system and the FV is between ±70 sec/qt so this mud system can be diluted with
water after drill 65 meter below ground level for this thinks mentioned your plan for pumping Hi Vis sweep.

           26" HOLE SIZE                          20" Casing Size
        FROM : Surface                   TO : 150 MT
           150 meter to drill in Aghajari Gel mud (Bentonite mud)
          DRILLING FLUID SYSTEM                      Drill Water Sweep by Gel Mud Pill

          Key products                                Bentonite , Caustic , Soda ash

           Water type                                               Drill Water + Waste Water
             Solid Control                             Shale shakers

             Aghajari               :            Mainly Limestone inter bed upward with Marl and Gypsum

Interval Discussion

Gel Mud system will be simply formulated with Bentonite to obtain the required suspending properties and form a good
filter cake. Makeup water should be checked and treated with SODA ASH to treat out hardness as needed. Adequate
surface volume should be prepared in advance, allowing maximum pre-hydration time.
All solids control equipment should be serviced and brought to optimum operating condition.
Run all solids control equipment continuously while circulating.
The mud density has to be carefully monitored and adapted to the well conditions, it is suggested to maintain the mud
weight at 65 - 67 pcf according to the well stability, this will be done by running the solids control equipment, control and
dump settled drilled solids from sand trap and diluting the active system with new mixed mud as required.
Due to the large hole size, the hole should be swept frequently with HV pills made by Gel Mud (Viscosity: 70 - 80 sec/lt).
Losses may occur while drilling this top section specially between surface up to 50 mt which for prevent this matter it is
recommended to add 4 ppb Mica M and 4 PPB Mica C together. Mud should be treated with SODIUM BICARBONATE
before cement job to prevent cement contamination.
It is recommended that a high viscosity pill be spotted on bottom prior to running the casing.

               Product N.                 lb/bbl        Product Function
                    Bentonite                    20 - 25             Viscosifier

17 1/2” Section from 150 to 1276 m

The 17 ½" section will be drilled from 20" Casing Shoe @ ±150 m to ±800 mt with previous mud in hole (Gel mud) then
at depth will be displace by KCL/Polymer mud and drill to an estimated section TD 1272.4 m.
At this system uses Salt/KCl as the source of density and Clay inhibition in the system. Mud weight will be Increase to
88 pcf by Barite as weighting agent; Rheology will be controlled by the use of Xanthan gum. Also Fluid loss will be keep
under control by the addition of polymeric chemicals such as, Starch LV. Addition of Drilling Detergent (As contingency)

                                            TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:      P | 91

────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                         Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

   Contract No.        Co.            Prj.           Dis.        Dep.        Doc.        Rev.         Well No.
  CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZN     PM      ENG     WDP        00      G21(SAZNXXX)

and KCl (3 % wt.) to the mud system can prevent bit balling and consequently can help to optimize the drilling operation
while drilling sticky Marl and Clay section. Fluid loss should be kept below 10 cc, meanwhile.
Also pH should be maintained within 9 - 9.5 while drilling Marl and keep within 9.5 – 10 while drilling Anhydrite.
At this section for the inhibition of system must be use PHPA (0.25 ppb) which adheres to the surface of the drilled
cuttings, encapsulating them, and also coating the well bore reducing the hydration of clays in the formation.

           17 1/2" HOLE SIZE                      13 3/8" Casing Size
        FROM : 150 MT                    TO : 1276 MT
            1122.4 meter to drill in Aghajari & Gachsaran ( Cap rock ) by KCl/Polymer mud
          DRILLING FLUID SYSTEM               KCl /Polymer Mud
                                                             Barite , Salt (NaCl ) , KCL , Xanthan gum  ,
          Key products                               Starch LV , Caustic Soda, Soda ash, PHPA ,
                                                         Biocide
           Water type                                               Drill Water + Waste Water
                                                    Shale shakers , Desilters , Desander,
             Solid Control                                                          Centrifuge

Interval Mud Discussion
After drilling shoe track and rate hole with carried Bentonite mud from previous section KCl/Polymer mud will be employed
to drilling this hole section.
Saturated Salt Muds are primarily used to prevent excessive hole enlargement while drilling massive salt beds. They can
also be used to reduce dispersion and hydration of shales and clays.
Potassium chloride is the primary source of the potassium ions in this system. In case of soft, sticky red Marl & Clay which
encountered problems such as bit balling additions of drilling KCl (3 %) to inhibit clays in Aghajari is recommended.
Potassium is used in areas where inhibition is required to limit chemical alteration of shales. Potassium performance is
based on cationic exchange of potassium for sodium or calcium ions on smectites and interlayered clays. Potassium-
based muds perform best on shales containing large quantities of smectite or interlayered clays in the total clay fraction.
Shallow shales, containing large amounts of montmorillonite.
Potassium interactions with clay surfaces can be traced to two effects: ionic size and hydration energy. Potassium ions
are of the proper size to fit snugly into the spaces between the two silica tetrahedral layers which contact each other in
the formation of a three-layer clay packet. The ionic diameter of potassium is 2.66 Å, very close to the available distance
of 2.8 Å in the lattice space of the clay structure. A cation slightly smaller than 2.8 Å is desirable to allow for crystalline
compaction.
In addition of using KCl, despite Aghajari is an un-permeable formation and seems that isn’t necessary to control Fluid
loss, Starch and PAC LV may be used to keep FL under control.
While drilling this section Xanthan gum will be used as base of rheology to extend hole cleaning and providing suitable
YP and LSRV. An YP between 15-20 lb/100ft2 for sufficient carrying capacity and a LSRYP at range of 7-15 to preventing
any Barite sag in system is required.
As it is seen in geological forecast, soft & sticky marls referred as Gumbo, contained large amount of Smectite and/or Illite
could be associated with problems such as bit balling, mud rings, plugged flow lines and hole washout& cause low process
of drilling and unnecessary trips and some more difficulties to drill the section as fast as possible. With all those problems
reduction of ROP was inevitable, blinding of shaker screens, excessive over pull on trips, washout and stuck pipes has
been experienced as well. For problem Solve can add 3-5 % KCl in system.
In case of necessary the inhibition of system can be increased by adding PHPA which adheres to the surface of the drilled
cuttings, encapsulating them, and also coating the well bore reducing the hydration of clays in the formation.

                                            TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:      P | 92

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                         Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

   Contract No.        Co.            Prj.           Dis.        Dep.        Doc.        Rev.         Well No.
  CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZN     PM      ENG     WDP        00      G21(SAZNXXX)

Also while drilling this section encounter with Ca2+ based Gypsum is inventible. Gyp is calcium sulfate with water of
crystallization (CaSO4.2H2O), Calcium sulfate contamination is similar to cement contamination because both liberate
calcium ions, which in turn cause flocculation. Unlike cement, calcium sulfate does not because a pH increase since it
supplies a sulfate ion in lieu of a hydroxyl ion. The sulfate ion contributes to flocculation of clay solids, although its effect
is small compared to calcium ions. The severity of flocculation depends to a great degree on clay content. When CaSO4
causes the calcium concentration to increase and viscosity may fluctuate drastically. As calcium ion concentrations further
increase, a base exchange occurs in which sodium montmorillonite becomes calcium montmorillonite. There are several
methods for handling CaSO4 contamination. The drilling fluid can be maintained as a low-calcium fluid by chemically
precipitating by adding soda ash.

         KCL / Polymer mud
           Product N.              Ppb                 Product Function
            Caustic Soda                0.25 – 0.5                 Alkalinity Control
           Lime                         0.5 - 1                     Alkalinity Control
          Soda Ash                 1 – 2                 Hardness Remover

                                                             Encapsulate
         PHPA                   As Requirement

             Biocide                  As Requirement         Anti-bacteria
          MIXING PROCEDURE

              1. PH , chloride , calcium and total hardness of drill water ( that water which will be used for
         mud making ) must be check by Mud engineer

              2. Fill the mud tank with Drill water
              3. add KCL as inhibitor
              4. add Salt to reach mentioned mud weight
              5. Remove Hardness by add Soda ash
              6. adjust PH & alkalinity by Caustic soda and Lime
              7. add Starch LV to control fluid loss
              8. add Xanthan gum to reach good rheology
              9. Add Barite powder to reach Mentioned Weight

Interval Mud Discussion
After drilling shoe track and rate hole fresh KCl/Polymer mud will be employed to drilling this hole section.
Saturated Salt Muds are primarily used to prevent excessive hole enlargement while drilling massive salt beds. They can
also be used to reduce dispersion and hydration of shales and clays.
Potassium chloride is the primary source of the potassium ions in this system. In case of soft, sticky red Marl & Clay which
encountered problems such as bit balling additions of drilling KCl (3 %) to inhibit clays in Pabdeh is recommended.
Potassium is used in areas where inhibition is required to limit chemical alteration of shales. Potassium performance is
based on cationic exchange of potassium for sodium or calcium ions on smectites and interlayered clays. Potassium-
based muds perform best on shales containing large quantities of smectite or interlayered clays in the total clay fraction.
Shallow shales, containing large amounts of montmorillonite.
Potassium interactions with clay surfaces can be traced to two effects: ionic size and hydration energy. Potassium ions
are of the proper size to fit snugly into the spaces between the two silica tetrahedral layers which contact each other in
the formation of a three-layer clay packet. The ionic diameter of potassium is 2.66 Å, very close to the available distance
of 2.8 Å in the lattice space of the clay structure. A cation slightly smaller than 2.8 Å is desirable to allow for crystalline
compaction.
In addition of using KCl, despite Aghajari is an un-permeable formation and seems that isn’t necessary to control Fluid
loss, Starch and PAC LV may be used to keep FL under control.
While drilling this section Xanthan gum will be used as base of rheology to extend hole cleaning and providing suitable
YP and LSRV. An YP between 15-20 lb/100ft2 for sufficient carrying capacity and a LSRYP at range of 7-15 to preventing
any Barite sag in system is required.
As it is seen in geological forecast, soft & sticky marls referred as Gumbo, contained large amount of Smectite and/or Illite
could be associated with problems such as bit balling, mud rings, plugged flow lines and hole washout& cause low process

                                            TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:      P | 97

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                         Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

   Contract No.        Co.            Prj.           Dis.        Dep.        Doc.        Rev.         Well No.
  CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZN     PM      ENG     WDP        00      G21(SAZNXXX)

of drilling and unnecessary trips and some more difficulties to drill the section as fast as possible. With all those problems
reduction of ROP was inevitable, blinding of shaker screens, excessive over pull on trips, washout and stuck pipes has
been experienced as well. For problem Solve can add 3 % KCl in system.
In case of necessary the inhibition of system can be increased by adding PHPA which adheres to the surface of the drilled
cuttings, encapsulating them, and also coating the well bore reducing the hydration of clays in the formation.
Also while drilling this section encounter with Ca2+ based Gypsum is inventible. Gyp is calcium sulfate with water of
crystallization (CaSO4.2H2O), Calcium sulfate contamination is similar to cement contamination because both liberate
calcium ions, which in turn cause flocculation. Unlike cement, calcium sulfate does not because a pH increase since it
supplies a sulfate ion in lieu of a hydroxyl ion. The sulfate ion contributes to flocculation of clay solids, although its effect
is small compared to calcium ions. The severity of flocculation depends to a great degree on clay content. When CaSO4
causes the calcium concentration to increase and viscosity may fluctuate drastically. As calcium ion concentrations further
increase, a base exchange occurs in which sodium montmorillonite becomes calcium montmorillonite. There are several
methods for handling CaSO4 contamination. The drilling fluid can be maintained as a low-calcium fluid by chemically
precipitating by adding soda ash.

Product Concentration and Function

         KCL / Polymer mud
           Product N.              Ppb                 Product Function
            Caustic Soda                 0.5 - 1                     Alkalinity Control
          Soda Ash                 1 – 2                 Hardness Remover
              Salt                     91 - 109             non - solid weighting agent
         KCL                        10.7                  Shale Inhibitor
            Limestone Powder         Up to 166              weighting agent
            Starch LV                 12 – 14                  Fluid loss controller
           Xanthan gum                 1-1.5                     Viscosifier
             Biocide                  As Requirement         Anti-bacteria
          MIXING PROCEDURE

              1. PH , chloride , calcium and total hardness of drill water ( that water which will be used for
         mud making ) must be check by Mud engineer

              2. Fill the mud tank with Drill water
              3. add KCL as inhibitor
              4. add Salt to reach mentioned mud weight
              5. Remove Hardness by add Soda ash
              6. adjust PH & alkalinity by Caustic soda
              7. add Starch LV to control fluid loss
              8. add Xanthan gum to reach good rheology
              9. Add Limestone powder to reach Mentioned Weight
           10.Check the pH and keep in range by add caustic soda

4.5.8 Waste Management

 Introduction
The waste water and drilling fluids waste treatment is the main important part of the services that should be consider more
to minimize the waste treatment cost. In addition, by recycling the water phase of the waste, we reduce the water
consumption and recover soluble chemicals. This reduces the cost of material consumptions and the cost of dumping
waste water at site. This process is usually done by using a chemically mechanically method to separate drilling solids
and recycle water. This process is usually done by using a high-speed centrifuges or filtering equipment and chemical
dosing system. This process is very efficient in drilling site especially in locations that water supply is the main problem.
Chemical Precipitation is the most common technology used to remove the dissolved particles from solutions. The ionic
particles are converted to an insoluble form (particle) by the chemical reaction between the soluble compounds and the
precipitating reagent. The particles formed by this reaction are removed from solution by settling, filtration or other
separation equipment. The unit operations typically required in this technology includes neutralization, precipitation,
coagulation/ flocculation, solids/liquid separation.
Cuttings treatment is important part of the waste management services which is usually done to solidification and stabilize
and fix the liquid phase movement on the solids. In this treatment, material is added to reduce free water and possibly
reduce or slow potential contaminant release. The treated cuttings will be transferred to environmentally designated area
specified by client.
All related services such as design, supply, execute, supervise and control of waste water and waste mud treatment plan
was performed according to the industry standards. The above-mentioned waste treatment operations performed under
and complied with Iranian Petroleum Standards (IPS) as well as good engineering practices in oil and exploration industry.

                                       Zero Discharge Plan (During and after Drilling Operation)

 PDF Waste Management Services Policy

PDF offers a wide range of waste management services to minimize generated waste and contaminated discharged
cuttings during drilling operations. This helps keep the environment clean and ensures that our operations are in
compliance with DOE (Department of Environment) legislations.
We provide cost-effective and optimum solutions from environmentally acceptable procedures to achieve zero-discharge
plans and improve fluids recovery. The outcome reduces operational costs and enables recycling.
Wastes are analyzed for various parameters such as chloride, nitrate, phosphate, BOD (Biological Oxygen Demand),
COD (Chemical Oxygen Demand) and oil content. Other analysis is carried out to monitor treatment processes on a
regular basis during waste generation. Our waste management personnel are fully trained, experienced and have the
competencies in place to provide the best reliable services with concentrated support and the least liabilities.

  Drilling Waste Management Steps

 Waste Treatment Plan
The drilling waste management plan for drilling operation is based on the major produced wastes during the drilling
operation. Due to the environmental restriction, it is important to monitor the chemical elements not to be discharged to
the ground water.

Seven items have been considered during the drilling to protect the environment as follow:
   ▪   Drilling Fluids Waste
   ▪  Waste Water
   ▪   Oily Waste Water
   ▪  Cement Slurry Waste
   ▪   Drilling Cuttings Waste
   ▪  Water Discharge Area (Waste Pit Design and Protection)
   ▪   Cuttings Discharge Area
According to drilling fluid, drilling waste divided in 2 major category, Waste management plan will be design for Water
Based Mud (WBM) or Oil Based Mud (OBM)

 Waste Water Treatment
The waste water and drilling fluids waste treatment is the main important part of the services that should be consider more
to minimize the waste treatment cost. In addition, by recycling the water phase of the waste, we reduce the water
consumption and recover soluble chemicals. This reduces the cost of material consumptions and the cost of dumping
waste water at site.
This process is usually done by using a chemically mechanically method to separate drilling solids and recycle water. This
process is usually done by using a high-speed centrifuges or filtering equipment and chemical dosing system. This process
is very efficient in drilling site especially in locations that water supply is the main problem.

 Cuttings Treatment Process
Cutting’s treatment is important part of the waste management services which is usually done to solidification and stabilize
and fix the liquid phase movement on the solids.

Solidification refers to techniques that encapsulate the waste in a monolithic solid of high structural integrity. In this
treatment, material is added to reduce free water and possibly reduce or slow potential contaminant release. Contaminant

migration is restricted by vastly decreasing the surface area exposed to leaching by isolating the wastes within an
impervious capsule.

Stabilization refers to those techniques that reduce the hazard potential of wastes by converting contaminants into their
least soluble, mobile, or toxic form. The physical nature and handling characteristics of the waste are not necessarily
changed by stabilization.

 Dewatering process Advantages

  ✓ 60% to 85% Recovery of the aqueous phases from the waste mud
  ✓ 40% reduction of waste volume and transportation.
  ✓  The water phase contained into the waste volume is almost totally recovered and re-sent back to the rig for new
      mud mixing, rig activities etc. This will drastically reduce the cost of transportation for the water needed for
        operations.
  ✓  Reduction in waste pit size and smallest foot print
       Produced water should be tested for different ions.

 Flocculation Unit
Chemical Precipitation is the most common technology used to remove the dissolved particles from solutions. The ionic
particles are converted to an insoluble form (particle) by the chemical reaction between the soluble compounds and the
precipitating reagent. The particles formed by this reaction are removed from solution by settling, filtration or other
separation equipment. The unit operations typically required in this technology includes neutralization, precipitation,
coagulation/ flocculation, solids/liquid separation. The flocculation unit is comprises three main parts;

    1-  Chemical Mixing Area                                                               Charge
                                                                                                             Neutralization
    2-  Chemical Dosing Unit                                                                                      Like  charged  particles
                                                                                                                           repel removal of charge
    3-  Control Unit                                                                                   can enable particles to
                                                                                                          approach close enough
                                                                                                                           to coagulate

                                                                            Bridging
                                                                                 Flocculation
                                                                                        Polymer chains can
                                                                                                    "bridge" particles to
                                                                                                  create larger masses

                                                                                Flocculation
                                                                               Process

 Laboratory Test Kits

 Spectrophotometry

Spectrophotometry uses photometers that can
measure a light beam's intensity as a function of
its     color     (wavelength)    known    as
spectrophotometers.   Important   features   of
spectrophotometers are spectral bandwidth, (the
range of colors  it can transmit through the test
sample),  and  the  percentage  of  sample-
transmission,  and  the  logarithmic  range  of
sample-absorption and sometimes a percentage
of reflectance measurement.
A spectrophotometer is commonly used for the
measurement  of  transmittance  or  reflectance  of
solutions,  transparent  or opaque  solids,  such  as
polished glass, or gases

 Conductivity Meter

The common  laboratory  conductivity meters
employ  a  potentiometric  method  and  four
electrodes. Often, the electrodes are cylindrical
and arranged concentrically. The electrodes are
usually made of platinum metal. An alternating
current  is applied  to  the  outer  pair  of  the
electrodes. The potential between the inner pair
is measured. Conductivity could in principle be
determined using the distance between the
electrodes and  their surface area using the
Ohm’s Law but generally, for accuracy, a calibration is
employed   using    electrolytes    of   well-known
conductivity.

                                       TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY:     P | 124

────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                         Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

   Contract No.        Co.            Prj.           Dis.        Dep.        Doc.        Rev.         Well No.
  CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZN     PM      ENG     WDP        00      G21(SAZNXXX)

 pH Meter

A pH Meter is a scientific instrument that measures the hydrogen-ion concentration (or
pH) in a solution, indicating  its acidity or alkalinity. The pH meter measures the
difference in electrical potential between a pH electrode and a reference electrode.
Knowledge of pH to greater or lesser accuracy is useful or critical in a great many
situations, including of course chemical laboratory work. pH meters of various types
and quality can be used for soil measurements in agriculture; water quality for water
supply systems, swimming pools, etc.; brewing, industrially or domestically; healthcare,
to ensure that solutions are safe when applied to patients or lethal as storylands and
disinfectants; and many other applications. It has measurements from 0 to 14

 Titration Method

Titration, also known as titrimetric, is a common laboratory method
of quantitative chemical analysis that  is used to determine the
unknown concentration  of an  identified analyze. Since volume
measurements play a key role in  titration,  it  is also known as
volumetric analysis. A reagent, called the  titrant or  titration  is
prepared as a standard solution. A known concentration and volume
of titrant reacts with a solution of analyze or titrant to determine
concentration. The volume of titrant reacted is called titration volume
